Comment analysis · episodes 1–100 · blocks of 10

Wolves of Blood Moon: what listeners complain about, block by block

I tagged each of the 1384 WOBM comments and 1963 TAB comments on episodes 1–100 with one main problem, then compared every 10-episode block against The Alpha's Bride (TAB). Red cells mark the problems WOBM has more of than the benchmark.

50%WOBM comments that raise a problem (TAB 76%)
13%WOBM comments that praise (TAB 7%)
340comments on WOBM eps 1–10, 2.1× any later block
3blocks where sexual or disturbing content tops 10% (TAB: none)

WOBM draws fewer complaints than TAB overall. TAB's listeners pile onto Talia's dirty, aged look and story contradictions for most of the first 100 episodes. WOBM's trouble comes in sharper spikes: the voice in the first 10 episodes, and three scenes that read as assault or underage sex.

The 5 cells doing the most damage

Ranked by how far WOBM sits above TAB in the same block.

1

Eps 1–10 · Voice / narration

One female narrator voices every alpha

23.8%of WOBM comments (81 of 340)
8.6%TAB, same block
+15.3points above benchmark

Listeners arrive from a Facebook or Instagram ad that used separate male and female voices. In the app, one woman narrates everyone, including the four alphas. 14% of all eps 1–10 comments name the ad, against 8% on TAB.

Spikes at ep 1, 2, 5 and 10. Ep 5 alone drew 19 voice complaints.

“If you are already using Ai voices, why not use different voices for each character and especially a male voice for male characters?”
“This isn’t the voice from the Facebook story! What happened?, this is horrible!”
2

Eps 41–50 · Sexual / disturbing content

Ep 42 reads as non-consent

15.5%of WOBM comments (25 of 161)
1.1%TAB, same block
+14.5points above benchmark

Violet says no over and over while Asher keeps going. Listeners call it molestation and abuse. The same eps carry a second spike: 8% of comments turn on Asher as a predator.

Ep 42 drew 10 of the block's 25 discomfort comments, ep 41 drew 4.

“This is disturbing. It's not a full physical rape but it is molestation. She has said "No. Leave me alone. Get away from me. Get off me." over and over”
“Mates or married or dating...doesn't matter. No means no. She never gave consent.”
3

Eps 31–40 · Sexual / disturbing content

Ep 35's wolf-licking welcome scene

13.2%of WOBM comments (20 of 151)
0.0%TAB, same block
+13.2points above benchmark

The pack licks Violet as her school welcome. Listeners read it as sexual assault and bestiality. TAB's eps 31–40 drew zero comments of this kind.

Ep 35 drew 12 of the block's 20 discomfort comments.

“That all scene was disturbing… Was it really part of welcome their humans into the school?? Or only her?”
“Aaand this was where I lost interest in the story because wtaf?! This chapter made me want to throw up.”
4

Eps 91–100 · Sexual / disturbing content

Adult with teenagers, and too much sex

11.1%of WOBM comments (10 of 90)
0.6%TAB, same block
+10.5points above benchmark

Listeners object to an adult character and teenage girls in ep 99, and say the sex now crowds out the plot. Some ask for an age restriction on the app.

Ep 99 is the peak, with eps 94–100 close behind.

“Somebody is going to catch a statutory charge from this nonsense”
“I'm done with this book, its just porn and has no substance or plot”
5

Eps 21–30 · Characters look bad

Asher looks like a zombie

19.9%of WOBM comments (30 of 151)
10.3%TAB, same block
+9.5points above benchmark

The script sells Asher as the hot alpha. On screen, listeners see a gaunt, old, creepy man with a speech impediment. The story's romance and the image pull apart.

Eps 26–28 hold 22 of the block's 30 looks complaints. Ep 27 alone has 10.

“whyy does he look like a dehydrated vampire”
“There is nothing with the current visual of Asher that is raw, strong or dominant. Who chose this image?”

Top fixes for WOBM, eps 1–100

I grouped WOBM's complaint comments into specific, fixable feedback, one fix per comment. The number shows how many comments back each fix, and the bars show where in eps 1–100 those comments land. Click a fix to read every comment behind it.

Top 5 problems per block, as a share of all comments in that block. Grey figure = TAB in the same block.

Eps 1–10

340 comments · 73% complaints · TAB 64%

Biggest pile of comments in the show (340, more than double any other block) and the highest complaint share (73%). Voice leads at 24%, then format at 12%: video drops to still shots, captions fail, and some listeners landed here from a The Alpha's Bride ad. Character looks (9%), quitting (7%) and redesign anger (5%: "they changed Violet and the alphas") follow. Most of it traces back to the ad: the episodes don't sound or look like what people clicked.

How I built this

  • Source: the two in-app comment exports (10,000 latest comments each). WOBM comments run 1 Aug – 8 Oct 2026, TAB 29 Aug – 8 Oct 2026. I kept every comment on episodes 1–100.
  • Seven Sonnet agents read and tagged the comments by hand against a fixed 15-label list (12 problems plus praise, plot chat and other), one main label per comment. They saw comment text only, never the show name. I spot-checked 42 random labels across all seven batches and found none misaligned.
  • Shares use all comments in the block as the base, so a block's problem shares plus praise and chat add to 100%.
  • Small blocks swing hard. WOBM eps 61–100 have 76–90 comments each, so one comment moves a share by over 1 point. Read gaps under 3 points as noise.
  • This measures what listeners say, not who leaves. Pair it with the episodic retention export to check which spikes cost listeners.
